| **[Delaunay-Based Nearest Neighbor Search with Grid-Accelerated Queries and Parallel Construction](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.15320v1)** | 2026-11-11 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>Nearest-neighbor search is a fundamental primitive in computer graphics, with applications in distance field computation, surface reconstruction, and point cloud registration. In many graphics applications, data points lie on a surface manifold embedded in 3D space, whereas queries are distributed throughout the surrounding volume, a setting in which spatial partitioning structures such as k-d trees and grids can become inefficient. Dynamic-programming-based nearest-neighbor search (DP-NNS) offers an alternative: it traverses a successor DAG that records the history of incremental Delaunay construction and often outperforms spatial partitioning structures. However, its sequential construction is slow, and naive parallelization degrades query performance, limiting its practical adoption. We accelerate both queries and construction in DP-NNS. For queries, we introduce a grid-based initialization strategy that precomputes valid starting points on the successor DAG, shortening search paths while preserving exactness and enabling a twofold speedup. For construction, we analyze how parallel insertion affects query cost and develop a parallel algorithm that preserves logarithmic expected query cost under stated geometric assumptions. Our parallel construction algorithm achieves a 10x speedup over the sequential baseline while maintaining nearly the same query cost. We further reorder query points to improve cache locality. Extensive experiments demonstrate that our method outperforms widely used libraries in nearest-neighbor query performance.</p></details> |  |
| **[A Multi-Parent Edge Assembly Crossover Driven Hybrid Genetic Algorithm for the Hamiltonian \textit{p}-Median Problem](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.15219v1)** | 2026-11-11 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>The Hamiltonian \textit{p}-median problem seeks to partition the vertices of a graph into exactly $p$ disjoint cycles while minimizing their total cost. This paper presents a hybrid genetic algorithm incorporating a multi-parent edge assembly crossover operator. The crossover combines a base solution with multiple donor solutions in a single recombination operation, thereby expanding the space of available edge exchanges. It uses virtual copies of the base solution to establish edge balance, together with an auxiliary directed graph and a budgeted search to construct valid AB-cycles. Extensive experiments on 215 benchmark instances across four sets show that the proposed algorithm improves the best-known solution values for 34 instances and matches those for another 168. Additional experiments indicate that a moderate number of parents provides beneficial diversity, whereas excessive parent counts, particularly when $p$ is large, lead to excessive population dispersion and hinder convergence.</p></details> |  |
| **[ViGA-FLAME: Aligning Latent Visual and Geometric Representations for Robust 3D Head Expression Estimation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.14218v1)** | 2026-11-10 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>Estimating 3D head expression from a single image requires mapping visual appearance to underlying facial geometry. We propose ViGA-FLAME, a two-stage framework that aligns semantic visual features with a structured geometric latent space. First, a Parametric-Geometric Variational Autoencoder (PG-VAE) learns a unified representation of canonical head surfaces and FLAME parameters, supporting both detailed surface reconstruction and interpretable parameter recovery. Supervision on geometry and parameters, together with consistency between the two decoding branches, connects surface detail with parametric structure. Second, a trainable Visual-Geometric Adapter (ViGA) maps features from a pretrained semantic visual encoder into this latent space. Asymmetric attention enables ViGA to align semantic image features from the visual encoder with the structured geometric information represented in the PG-VAE latent space, without altering the original visual stream. By grounding image evidence in this latent prior, ViGA-FLAME enables robust 3D head expression estimation across severe facial deformations and large viewing angles, efficiently improving robustness over competing feed-forward methods.</p></details> |  |
| **[DepthGS: Depth-Guided Gaussian Splatting with Adaptive Regularization for High-Quality Surface Reconstruction](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.13628v1)** | 2026-11-10 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>Recently, 3D Gaussian Splatting (3DGS) has emerged as an efficient representation for novel view synthesis, offering fast differentiable rendering and high visual fidelity, and has increasingly been extended to surface reconstruction. However, the relationship between depth information and reconstruction error remains insufficiently explored, limiting the principled design of geometric optimization strategies. To fill the above gap, we propose DepthGS, a depth-guided Gaussian splatting framework. We derive theoretical relations between depth discrepancies and reconstruction error under single-view and multi-view settings, and rigorously establish corresponding upper bounds on reconstruction error. The above analysis informs the design of adaptive regularization strategies in both single-view and multi-view optimization. More specifically, for single-view optimization, we propose a depth-difference-guided adaptive normal regularization strategy that modulates the normal constraint strength using a geometric reliability weight computed from local depth differences. For multi-view optimization, we introduce a cross-view depth consistency regularization strategy that promotes consistency between neighboring-view depth maps and the corresponding depth predictions obtained by transforming the current view's depth predictions into those neighboring views. Extensive experiments on DTU, Mip-NeRF360, and Tanks and Temples demonstrate that DepthGS achieves competitive performance in both surface reconstruction and novel view synthesis.</p></details> | To appear in IEEE Trans. on Pattern Analysis and Machine Intelligence |
| **[An exact compress-with-another threshold in a binary automaton family](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.07044v2)** | 2026-11-10 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>For a state $q$ in a synchronizing automaton, let $μ(q)$ be the minimum length of a word merging $q$ with another initial state. Szykuła asked whether $μ(q)=O(n)$ for strongly connected synchronizing automata and described a binary family attributed to Dżyga, with $n=3p$ states and a conjectured threshold $4n/3$. We specify the boundary transitions omitted from the published piecewise definition and prove that the resulting automata $\mathcal A_p$ satisfy $μ(q_0)=4p=4n/3$ for all $p\geq3$. A shortest word merging $q_0$ with another state is $(ba)^p(ab)^p$. We also construct a reset word of length $3p^2+4p-1$. Consequently, any uniform linear upper bound for $μ(q)$ on binary strongly connected synchronizing automata with corank-one letters has leading constant at least $4/3$.</p></details> | 9 pages, 1 figure. Revised proofs and exposition, corrected boundary transitions, updated references, and added a coauthor |

## neural rendering
| **Title** | **Date** | **Abstract** | **Comment** |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| **[DAL: Dynamic Angular Loss for Imbalanced Medical Image Classification](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.24597v1)** | 2026-11-18 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>Class imbalance remains a major obstacle in medical image classification, where rare but clinically important classes are often overshadowed by majority categories. Although current strategies for reducing bias, including angle-based softmax (ABS) losses, offer strong discriminative features, their behavior in settings with a small number of severely imbalanced classes remains insufficiently understood. Previous approaches that estimate the intrinsic parameters of ABS rely on the assumption of many classes, which does not hold in medical settings. We derive data-driven upper and lower bounds for the scale parameter and an upper bound for the angular margin using negative-angle statistics, and use these to drive a batch-adaptive angular margin (BAAM), which we combine with dynamically-weighted cross entropy (DWCE) to define our dynamic angular loss (DAL). Across four highly imbalanced medical image classification benchmarks spanning dermatology, ophthalmology, and gastrointestinal endoscopy, our method consistently achieves superior balanced accuracy and the lowest coefficient of variation of per-class recall. Although this entails a small performance reduction on majority classes, the gains on minority categories are substantial, yielding a reliable and equitable overall classifier. Our source code is available at: https://github.com/msalman95/DAL.</p></details> | Accepted at the British Machine Vision Conference (BMVC) 2026. 18 pages, 5 figures, 4 tables. Code: https://github.com/msalman95/DA |
| **[LoC-IN: Real-Time Localization of Cameras in Neural Rendered Prior LiDAR Maps](https://arxiv.org/abs/2511.16349v2)** | 2026-11-16 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>Accurate camera localization is crucial for robotics and Extended Reality (XR), enabling reliable navigation and alignment of virtual and real content. Existing visual methods often suffer from drift, scale ambiguity, and depend on fiducials or loop closure. This work introduces a real-time method for localizing a camera within a pre-captured, highly accurate colored LiDAR point cloud. By rendering synthetic views from this cloud, 2D-3D correspondences are established between live frames and the point cloud. A neural rendering technique narrows the domain gap between synthetic and real images, reducing occlusion and background artifacts to improve feature matching. The result is drift-free camera tracking with correct metric scale in the global LiDAR coordinate system. Two real-time variants are presented: Online Render and Match (R&amp;M), and Prebuild and Localize (P&amp;L). We demonstrate improved results on the ScanNet++ dataset and outperform existing SLAM pipelines.</p></details> | Published in IEEE Robotics and Automation Letters, 8 pages, 4 figures |
| **[TRUCO: A Scalable Lock-Free Algorithm for Parallel Contour Extraction](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.37297v3)** | 2026-11-15 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>Extracting binary contours and their containment hierarchy is a fundamental step in many computer vision pipelines. While the standard Suzuki--Abe algorithm is inherently sequential, existing parallel tracers neither preserve its exact contours nor compute their hierarchy. This paper presents TRUCO (Threaded Raster Unrestricted Contour Ownership), a lock-free parallel algorithm for multi-core CPUs that produces the exact output of Suzuki--Abe. TRUCO relies on two core ideas. First, the image is partitioned into horizontal stripes, and a geometric ownership rule allows each thread to trace complete contours independently across stripe boundaries, avoiding both locks and post-processing merges. Second, contour tracing is decoupled from hierarchy reconstruction, which is performed in a subsequent parallel pass and can be skipped entirely when only the contours are needed. We formally prove that TRUCO is deterministic, data-race-free, and identical in output to Suzuki--Abe regardless of thread count. Across diverse high-resolution datasets-from sparse segmentation masks to dense real-world photographs-TRUCO achieves speedups of 4.7x to 17.4x over the fastest sequential baseline with hierarchy computation, and 2.3x to 17.8x without it.</p></details> |  |
| **[Neural Render Proxies for Interactive and Differentiable Lighting](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.17185v1)** | 2026-11-12 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>Within the CG animation production pipeline, the challenges artists tackle in Lighting can be immense. Even minor adjustments require re-rendering massive scenes with slow offline renderers; global illumination has to be sampled and complex shaders have to be evaluated, leading to iteration times of minutes to hours per frame. To accelerate this process, we introduce a novel neural render proxy (NRP) that enables differentiable relighting of static scenes with fixed camera and materials at interactive rates. Our main insight is the decoupling of traditional rendering into path sampling and emission computation. From a single, light-agnostic render pass, we collect light transport data in the form of path samples. This enables rapidly sampling lighting conditions on the fly, and training a scene-specific lightweight neural network that learns how light is transported from any scene location to any image pixel. This approach is compatible with non-differentiable production renderers, induces minimal memory requirements during inference, and scales only with resolution and the number of light sources and parameters, but independently of scene and appearance complexity. Our evaluation demonstrates interactive frame rates for relighting (~30-60 Hz) while closely approximating the visual fidelity of ground-truth path tracing. In addition, the differentiable NRP enables fast, gradient-based inverse workflows, allowing artists to efficiently solve for lighting parameters from intuitive image-space edits or generative targets.</p></details> | Accepted at Computer Graphics Forum (Proc. EGSR 2026) |
| **[GC-MoE on SM120: Overlapping Expert-Parallel Token Dispatch with Warp-Level FP8xFP4 Block-Scaled MMA on Consumer Blackwell](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.16219v1)** | 2026-11-12 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>Expert-parallel Mixture-of-Experts decoding is latency bound: each layer serializes a token exchange, a grouped GEMM over routed experts, and a combine, while only a few tokens per rank are in flight. Prior GC-MoE fused these phases into one persistent, Green-Context-partitioned CUDA Graph on Hopper (SM90) and datacenter Blackwell (SM100). This paper rebuilds the operator for SM120 (compute capability 12.0, e.g. RTX PRO 5000) over PCIe. SM120 exposes block-scaled tensor cores only through warp-level mma.sync.aligned.kind::mxf8f6f4: no tensor memory, no two-CTA MMA, no cluster multicast. We rebuild the kernel around a swap-AB warp-level block-scaled MMA with FP4 weights in the register A operand and FP8 activations in B, plus SM120-specific mechanisms: scale-byte selection through MMA immediates, a compact-weights path, an adaptive token-tile geometry, an expert-granular streaming dispatch with a batched bulk-pull engine and a 64-bit deduplicating pull cache, full-width BF16 returns drained by resident relay copiers over a position-major arena, and shared experts folded into the compute role. On 8xRTX PRO 5000 over PCIe the operator is 1.522x and 1.387x faster than the FlashInfer CuTe DSL W4A8 MegaMoE and the Cake MegaMoE across 18 decode points (18/18 wins each), and 1.388x faster than Cake with shared experts fused; all 94 numerical cases and 752 per-rank checks pass, at cross-operator cosine similarity of at least 0.9999997.</p></details> | 24 pages, 10 tables, 6 figures |
| **[LILA: Data-Free Structured Pruning of LLMs via Latent Spectral Geometry](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.11163v2)** | 2026-11-11 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>Structured pruning of large language models (LLMs) has universally required calibration data, gradient computation, or large auxiliary policy networks at pruning time. LILA (\emph{Latent-Informed Layer Analysis}) demonstrates that this dependency is avoidable: each FFN weight matrix is factorized with Non-negative Matrix Factorization (NMF) to obtain a parts-based spectral representation, and neuron importance is scored via the Kolmogorov--Smirnov (KS) distance between singular value distributions of the full and neuron-ablated NMF matrix, a closed-form criterion requiring no calibration corpus, forward pass, or gradient computation. Two data-free matrix variants (Abs, Split) generate pruning masks from weight spectral geometry alone; a calibration-guided variant (Act) is provided as an internal ablation. Evaluation spans 7 model families (up to 70B). Without fine-tuning, data-free LILA surpasses PruneNet (45M-parameter RL policy) by up to 2.12~pp in zero-shot accuracy on LLaMA-2-7B and outperforms WikiText-2-calibrated SliceGPT by up to 6.1~pp across all sparsity levels, confirming data-free performance is not an artifact of omitting activation statistics. After one LoRA RFT epoch (matched WikiText-2 protocol), LILA Act surpasses SliceGPT+RFT by 6.39~pp on average using 98.4\% fewer calibration samples (16 vs.\ 1,024) across LLaMA-2-7B and Phi-2. NTK analysis confirms a $17{\times}$ reduction in functional distortion versus random pruning, providing theoretical grounding for the spectral criterion.</p></details> | 31 Pages |
| **[A Multi-Parent Edge Assembly Crossover Driven Hybrid Genetic Algorithm for the Hamiltonian \textit{p}-Median Problem](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.15219v1)** | 2026-11-11 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>The Hamiltonian \textit{p}-median problem seeks to partition the vertices of a graph into exactly $p$ disjoint cycles while minimizing their total cost. This paper presents a hybrid genetic algorithm incorporating a multi-parent edge assembly crossover operator. The crossover combines a base solution with multiple donor solutions in a single recombination operation, thereby expanding the space of available edge exchanges. It uses virtual copies of the base solution to establish edge balance, together with an auxiliary directed graph and a budgeted search to construct valid AB-cycles. Extensive experiments on 215 benchmark instances across four sets show that the proposed algorithm improves the best-known solution values for 34 instances and matches those for another 168. Additional experiments indicate that a moderate number of parents provides beneficial diversity, whereas excessive parent counts, particularly when $p$ is large, lead to excessive population dispersion and hinder convergence.</p></details> |  |
| **[CD-RCM: Generalizable Continuous-Depth Novel View Synthesis for Reflectance Confocal Microscopy](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.12635v2)** | 2026-11-10 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>Reflectance confocal microscopy (RCM) provides noninvasive, cellular-resolution "optical biopsies" of human skin in vivo by acquiring en-face images at successive depths, forming a sparse z-stack. Due to optical limitations, these stacks are anisotropic 3D volumes with lateral resolution (0.5 $μ$m) $\sim$6 times higher compared to axial resolution, which is defined by the optical sectioning (3 $μ$m), limiting the interpretation of tissue. Our goal is to provide continuous-depth visualization by interpolating intermediate sections and making the 3D volume isotropic. Such a representation permits arbitrary-direction sectioning, including histopathology-like cross-sectional examination, without requiring per-patient optimization. To that end, we introduce the first RCM-specific novel-view synthesis (NVS) approach, CD-RCM, a feedforward model that predicts realistic, unseen depths from sparsely sampled RCM stacks. Classical neural rendering methods focus on reconstruction from surface-level multi-view observations. In contrast to surface-level camera views, RCM can acquire optically sectioned en-face images of tissue beyond the surface up to 200 $μ$m. However, during visualization of the RCM stacks, observations of the shallower sections (towards the surface) obscure the deeper ones. This unique axial imaging geometry and layer-dependent anatomical organization motivated our development of a tailored architectural and training framework that explicitly accounts for RCM's depth-resolved, occlusive imaging physics. Experiments demonstrate that CD-RCM achieves high-fidelity novel-view synthesis with sub-second inference time.</p></details> |  |

## novel view synthesis
| **Title** | **Date** | **Abstract** | **Comment** |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| **[Multi-Band Galaxy Novel-View Synthesis with Radiative Gaussian Fields](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.24723v1)** | 2026-11-18 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>We propose a novel view synthesis tool for galaxies based on Gaussian Splatting (GS). Unlike RGB data, where GS has achieved success, galaxy observations consist of multi-band signals from a massive number of bodies, contain complex patterns of cross-band interactions, and capture light propagation in highly heterogeneous media. As a result, the underlying physical process poses great challenges in model representation and optimization, which have not been investigated in existing research. To this end, we propose a physically inspired Gaussian Splatting method, GalaxyGS, which is equipped with physically meaningful components that capture both the energy emission mechanism of different astronomical bodies and the light propagation between them. In the absence of directly comparable baseline methods, we adopt a wide range of possible alternative approaches. Comparisons conclusively demonstrate that GalaxyGS is better in accuracy and physical fidelity across datasets and metrics.</p></details> | 10 pages, 3 figures |
| **[DAL: Dynamic Angular Loss for Imbalanced Medical Image Classification](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.24597v1)** | 2026-11-18 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>Class imbalance remains a major obstacle in medical image classification, where rare but clinically important classes are often overshadowed by majority categories. Although current strategies for reducing bias, including angle-based softmax (ABS) losses, offer strong discriminative features, their behavior in settings with a small number of severely imbalanced classes remains insufficiently understood. Previous approaches that estimate the intrinsic parameters of ABS rely on the assumption of many classes, which does not hold in medical settings. We derive data-driven upper and lower bounds for the scale parameter and an upper bound for the angular margin using negative-angle statistics, and use these to drive a batch-adaptive angular margin (BAAM), which we combine with dynamically-weighted cross entropy (DWCE) to define our dynamic angular loss (DAL). Across four highly imbalanced medical image classification benchmarks spanning dermatology, ophthalmology, and gastrointestinal endoscopy, our method consistently achieves superior balanced accuracy and the lowest coefficient of variation of per-class recall. Although this entails a small performance reduction on majority classes, the gains on minority categories are substantial, yielding a reliable and equitable overall classifier. Our source code is available at: https://github.com/msalman95/DAL.</p></details> | Accepted at the British Machine Vision Conference (BMVC) 2026. 18 pages, 5 figures, 4 tables. Code: https://github.com/msalman95/DA |
| **[RAPID: Reconstruction Accelerated by Polarization-Informed Depth for 2D Gaussian Splatting](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.24404v1)** | 2026-11-18 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>Efficient and accurate 3D surface reconstruction from multi-view images remains a fundamental challenge in Gaussian splatting. Many existing methods rely on sparse geometric initialization and iterative densification, resulting in substantial optimization overhead and difficulties in balancing rendering quality and geometric accuracy, particularly in reflective and weakly textured regions. To address these limitations, we propose RAPID, a geometry-first framework that uses multiview polarization cues to recover dense surface priors. We integrate surface normals into depth maps using sparse geometric anchors. These depth maps are then fused across views. Curvature-adaptive sampling and oriented Gaussian initialization then support efficient optimization. Experiments on the PISR dataset demonstrate an approximately \(5\times\) speedup over PhyGaP, which is a state-of-the-art Gaussian-based reconstruction method. RAPID reduces the average optimization time from approximately 21 minutes (PhyGaP) to 4 minutes while maintaining competitive novel-view synthesis quality and mesh reconstruction accuracy. These results demonstrate the effectiveness of RAPID in accelerating Gaussian optimization from polarimetric multi-view inputs.</p></details> | 10 pages, 5 figures, 5tables |
| **[OctreeSplat: Hierarchical Octree-Guided Feed-Forward Gaussian Splatting](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.02658v3)** | 2026-11-18 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>3D Gaussian Splatting (3DGS) has shown considerable potential for novel view synthesis (NVS) and 3D reconstruction. Feed-forward methods avoid costly per-scene optimization, but fixed image or spatial resolutions limit their ability to allocate primitives according to local complexity. We present OctreeSplat, a hierarchical octree-guided feed-forward Gaussian splatting. OctreeSplat extracts multi-view features and geometric priors from a frozen visual foundation model (VFM), constructs a hierarchical octree, and predicts densification scores. The resulting representation allocates Gaussian primitives at spatially varying densities according to local complexity. Stochastic solids volume rendering improves depth rendering, strengthens geometric supervision, and supports surface reconstruction. When camera parameters are available, an optional test-time optimization (TTO) module improves agreement between predicted and ground-truth parameters. Training updates only 76 million additional parameters and requires 35 GPU-hours. Experiments on Replica, TUM, Tanks and Temples, and DTU demonstrate competitive RGB view synthesis, depth synthesis, and surface reconstruction. OctreeSplat reduces the number of Gaussian primitives while maintaining high reconstruction quality.</p></details> |  |
| **[WaveView: The Novel View Is in the Noise](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.23994v1)** | 2026-11-18 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>Generative novel view synthesis (NVS) employs a diffusion model to render a static or dynamic scene from a target camera view. Explicit warp methods complete a reprojection of the source with video diffusion models, and implicit methods encode the camera parameters or 3D cues. Both add inference overhead, and the generated layout often drifts from the ground truth. We present WaveView, which injects the warped source into the phase of the initial noise of a pretrained generator. The Dual-Tree Complex Wavelet Packet Transform (DT-CWPT) allows local and anisotropic phase injection. The generator is thus constrained less in disoccluded regions and along the epipolar line. WaveView keeps the architecture and adds negligible overhead. On the DL3DV benchmark, it outperforms all baselines in every overlap bin. In the hardest bin, it gains 0.84 dB PSNR on the covisible region over MetaView, which shares its generator, while running 2.1 times faster. For dynamic NVS, fine-tuning AnyView with the same noise raises its zero-shot PSNR on AnyViewBench from 11.58 to 12.35 dB.</p></details> |  |
| **[Structure-Aware Sparse-View X-ray 3D Reconstruction](https://arxiv.org/abs/2311.10959v4)** | 2026-11-18 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>X-ray, known for its ability to reveal internal structures of objects, is expected to provide richer information for 3D reconstruction than visible light. Yet, existing neural radiance fields (NeRF) algorithms overlook this important nature of X-ray, leading to their limitations in capturing structural contents of imaged objects. In this paper, we propose a framework, Structure-Aware X-ray Neural Radiodensity Fields (SAX-NeRF), for sparse-view X-ray 3D reconstruction. Firstly, we design a Line Segment-based Transformer (Lineformer) as the backbone of SAX-NeRF. Linefomer captures internal structures of objects in 3D space by modeling the dependencies within each line segment of an X-ray. Secondly, we present a Masked Local-Global (MLG) ray sampling strategy to extract contextual and geometric information in 2D projection. Plus, we collect a larger-scale dataset X3D covering wider X-ray applications. Experiments on X3D show that SAX-NeRF surpasses previous NeRF-based methods by 12.56 and 2.49 dB on novel view synthesis and CT reconstruction. Code, models, and data are released at https://github.com/caiyuanhao1998/SAX-NeRF . Our project page at https://caiyuanhao1998.github.io/project/SAX-NeRF/ shows more video and interactive results. An online Gradio demo is available on HuggingFace at https://huggingface.co/spaces/CaiYuanhao/SAX-NeRF</p></details> | CVPR 2024; The first Transformer-based method for X-ray and CT 3D reconstruction |
| **[EyeDiT: Anatomy-Grounded Multi-Modal Eye Generation and Simulation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.23670v1)** | 2026-11-18 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>Synthetic eye data are critical for rapid development of eye tracking (ET) in AR/VR applications. However, existing synthetic eye generators are largely image-centric and lack a high-quality underlying 3D anatomy of the eye, which limits their control mainly to the gaze and provides no anatomical labels. To address this problem, we propose EyeDiT, an anatomy-grounded, multi-modal 3D eye generation and simulation model trained with optical coherence tomography (OCT), labeled anatomy point clouds and near-eye images. Our pipeline first trains a Multi-Modal Anatomy Variational Autoencoder (VAE), which encodes an anatomy-aware latent representation from all input modalities. Then, our Eye Latent DiT is trained to generate latent samples conditioned on the optical axis, the pupil position and radius, and the subject identity. Together with Gaussian decoders for novel-view simulation, our model enables control over the pupil, subject identity and viewpoint for data synthesis. We show that our model outperforms previous eye image synthesis models in novel-view synthesis (24.9 against 21.8 dB PSNR from a single view), and that a public ET neural network trained on our synthetic data shows a smaller sim-to-real gap than when trained on data from other generators (3.5$^\circ$ vs. 8.4$^\circ$ gaze error), approaches a network trained on real data, and further improves ET performance when combined with real data (2.3$^\circ$ vs. 2.6$^\circ$). More information is available on the project page: https://lyhan12.github.io/eyedit</p></details> |  |
| **[Radiative Gaussian Splatting for Efficient X-ray Novel View Synthesis](https://arxiv.org/abs/2403.04116v4)** | 2026-11-17 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>X-ray is widely applied for transmission imaging due to its stronger penetration than natural light. When rendering novel view X-ray projections, existing methods mainly based on NeRF suffer from long training time and slow inference speed. In this paper, we propose a 3D Gaussian splatting-based method, namely X-Gaussian, for X-ray 3D reconstruction. Firstly, we redesign a radiative Gaussian point cloud model inspired by the isotropic nature of X-ray imaging. Our model excludes the influence of view direction when learning to predict the radiation intensity of 3D points. Based on this model, we develop a Differentiable Radiative Rasterization (DRR) with CUDA implementation. Secondly, we formulate a principled Radiative Gaussian Volume Querying (RGVQ) method that defines a continuous attenuation field from the original radiative point attributes and enables direct CT volume reconstruction through CUDA-based parallel querying. Thirdly, we customize an Angle-pose Cuboid Uniform Initialization (ACUI) strategy that directly uses the parameters of the X-ray scanner to compute the camera information and then uniformly samples point positions within a cuboid enclosing the scanned object. On novel view synthesis, X-Gaussian outperforms SOTA methods by 6.5 dB while enjoying less than 15% training time and over 73x inference speed. On CT reconstruction, X-Gaussian outperforms SOTA methods by 0.40 dB and 4.34 dB on synthetic and real scenes, while achieving 4.30x faster inference of the learned CT volume. Code, models, and datasets are at https://github.com/caiyuanhao1998/X-Gaussian . More video and interactive visualizations are presented on our project page at https://caiyuanhao1998.github.io/project/X-Gaussian . We also built an online Gradio demo page on HuggingFace at https://huggingface.co/spaces/CaiYuanhao/X-Gaussian .</p></details> | ECCV 2024; The first 3D Gaussian Splatting-based method for X-ray 3D reconstruction |
| **[TetraCoder: Adaptive Tetrahedral Encoding for Neural Fields](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.18267v3)** | 2026-11-17 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>Implicit and explicit representations of geometry are central in computer graphics applications and come with their pros and cons. With surface sampling being faster on explicit representations and spatial queries being faster on implicit representations, finding efficient conversions between them is a highly desirable property. We present TetraCoder, a single resolution hash-free adaptive tetrahedral encoding that links an explicit tetrahedral spatial encoder with an underlying implicit signed distance field (SDF). The resulting hybrid implicit-explicit space is continuous piecewise affine (CPWA) by design, allowing to obtain an exact correspondence between the implicit SDF and the explicit analytic mesh. TetraCoder's design comes with additional perks, in particular: (a) it scales better than multi-resolution encoding methods due to its single resolution optimizable grid, (b) unlike hash-based methods, it is not affected by collisions, (c) it enables fast tetrahedral ray-tracing, geometry and appearance editing, directly through its adaptive tetrahedral scaffold. We demonstrate the versatility of TetraCoder by showing an improvement over state-of-the-art methods on multiple tasks: 2D/3D fitting from images and point clouds, 3D novel view synthesis, 3D reconstruction, and geometry/appearance editing.</p></details> | 8 pages, 4 figures |

| **[DAL: Dynamic Angular Loss for Imbalanced Medical Image Classification](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.24597v1)** | 2026-11-18 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>Class imbalance remains a major obstacle in medical image classification, where rare but clinically important classes are often overshadowed by majority categories. LILA (\emph{Latent-Informed Layer Analysis}) demonstrates that this dependency is avoidable: each FFN weight matrix is factorized with Non-negative Matrix Factorization (NMF) to obtain a parts-based spectral representation, and neuron importance is scored via the Kolmogorov--Smirnov (KS) distance between singular value distributions of the full and neuron-ablated NMF matrix, a closed-form criterion requiring no calibration corpus, forward pass, or gradient computation. Two data-free matrix variants (Abs, Split) generate pruning masks from weight spectral geometry alone; a calibration-guided variant (Act) is provided as an internal ablation. Evaluation spans 7 model families (up to 70B). Without fine-tuning, data-free LILA surpasses PruneNet (45M-parameter RL policy) by up to 2.12~pp in zero-shot accuracy on LLaMA-2-7B and outperforms WikiText-2-calibrated SliceGPT by up to 6.1~pp across all sparsity levels, confirming data-free performance is not an artifact of omitting activation statistics. | **[BLaFA: Bridging Language to 3D Functional Affordances for Dexterous Robotic Grasping in Tool Use](https://arxiv.org/abs/2604.08410v4)** | 2026-11-11 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>In industrial assembly, equipment maintenance, and everyday tool-use scenarios, robots need not only to grasp objects stably but also to establish task-appropriate contact configurations according to task intentions and tool functionality. First, a Knowledge-guided Language Parsing (KLP) module leverages structured knowledge to transform natural-language instructions into semantic sextuples of manipulation constraints. Second, a 3D Gaussian Splatting based hierarchical semantic representation and a Triangular Functional Point Localization (TriLocation) module are developed to localize 3D functional contact keypoints under task-tool topology constraints. Finally, a 3D Keypoint Grasp Matrix Transformation (KGT3D+) module maps the localized keypoints and semantic constraints to executable wrist poses and finger-level joint and force commands. Evaluations on 100 open-vocabulary language instructions, 14 task-tool combinations covering four functional intents, and real-world robotic experiments in representative scenes demonstrate that BLaFA can effectively translate natural-language instructions into task-aligned 3D functional contact structures and corresponding dexterous grasps, thereby enabling language-driven robotic tool use across diverse tasks. The source code will be made publicly available. Code will be publicly available at https://github.com/PopeyePxx/BLaFA.</p></details> | Code will be publicly available at https://github.com/PopeyePxx/BLaFA |

| **[DAL: Dynamic Angular Loss for Imbalanced Medical Image Classification](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.24597v1)** | 2026-11-18 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>Class imbalance remains a major obstacle in medical image classification, where rare but clinically important classes are often overshadowed by majority categories. Although current strategies for reducing bias, including angle-based softmax (ABS) losses, offer strong discriminative features, their behavior in settings with a small number of severely imbalanced classes remains insufficiently understood. Previous approaches that estimate the intrinsic parameters of ABS rely on the assumption of many classes, which does not hold in medical settings. We derive data-driven upper and lower bounds for the scale parameter and an upper bound for the angular margin using negative-angle statistics, and use these to drive a batch-adaptive angular margin (BAAM), which we combine with dynamically-weighted cross entropy (DWCE) to define our dynamic angular loss (DAL). Across four highly imbalanced medical image classification benchmarks spanning dermatology, ophthalmology, and gastrointestinal endoscopy, our method consistently achieves superior balanced accuracy and the lowest coefficient of variation of per-class recall. Although this entails a small performance reduction on majority classes, the gains on minority categories are substantial, yielding a reliable and equitable overall classifier. Our source code is available at: https://github.com/msalman95/DAL.</p></details> | Accepted at the British Machine Vision Conference (BMVC) 2026. 18 pages, 5 figures, 4 tables. Code: https://github.com/msalman95/DA |
| **[OctreeSplat: Hierarchical Octree-Guided Feed-Forward Gaussian Splatting](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.02658v3)** | 2026-11-18 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>3D Gaussian Splatting (3DGS) has shown considerable potential for novel view synthesis (NVS) and 3D reconstruction. The resulting representation allocates Gaussian primitives at spatially varying densities according to local complexity. Stochastic solids volume rendering improves depth rendering, strengthens geometric supervision, and supports surface reconstruction. When camera parameters are available, an optional test-time optimization (TTO) module improves agreement between predicted and ground-truth parameters. Training updates only 76 million additional parameters and requires 35 GPU-hours. Experiments on Replica, TUM, Tanks and Temples, and DTU demonstrate competitive RGB view synthesis, depth synthesis, and surface reconstruction. OctreeSplat reduces the number of Gaussian primitives while maintaining high reconstruction quality.</p></details> |  |
| **[GSExplorer: Interactive World Generation with a Persistent 3DGS World State](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.01543v2)** | 2026-11-18 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>Interactive world generation requires both accurate local control and stable scene consistency over long rollouts. At each step, the current 3DGS scene provides multimodal rendering conditions for a video generator, while camera geometry is injected through a separate Relative Ray Encoding branch. We further design a data-construction pipeline that reproduces realistic 3DGS degradation during scene extrapolation, yielding 293K training pairs of degraded multimodal renderings and real target videos. GSExplorer achieves the best overall performance among the compared methods on both single-step and three-step WorldScore evaluation, including a Camera Control score of 99.07 in single-step extrapolation. To bridge these gaps, we propose VLN-AR, a hierarchical vision-language-navigation (VLN) framework for aerial robots. VLN-AR decouples multimodal reasoning and low-level control, enabling structured prediction of 3D pose commands and navigation intentions that are translated into dynamically feasible actions. To mitigate data scarcity and domain gaps, we introduce a fully automated 3D Gaussian Splatting (3DGS)-based pipeline to generate scalable photorealistic UAV navigation data. | **[Lifting 3D Gaussians: A Wavelet Multiresolution Representation for Scalable Gaussian Splatting Streaming](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.07179v2)** | 2026-11-18 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>3D Gaussian Splatting (3DGS) represents a scene as an unstructured set of primitives optimized at a single scale, offering no native support for progressive delivery to heterogeneous devices. | **[SSA-3DGS: Unsupervised Removal of Screen-Space Artifacts for 3D Gaussian Splatting](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.05598v3)** | 2026-11-17 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>Novel View Synthesis (NVS) methods, such as 3D Gaussian Splatting (3DGS), rely on the assumption of clean, multi-view consistent, posed input images. | **[MACRO: Training-free Multi-plane Attention for Closeup Render Optimization](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.03875v2)** | 2026-11-14 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>Close-up rendering, zooming into a scene well beyond any training camera, is important for virtual production and interactive 3D content, yet remains an open challenge. 3D Gaussian splatting (3DGS) enables high-fidelity, real-time novel view synthesis, but its rendering quality degrades at close range. Recent diffusion-based methods that enhance the rendering by conditioning on reference images from the training set produce significant artifacts in this setting. We analyze this failure and identify its root cause: the scale gap between the close-up and reference views. We show that the features in reference-conditioned enhancement models are not scale-invariant, causing cross-view attention to retrieve incorrect correspondences when the same content appears at different scales, and that this mismatch cannot be corrected in latent space because the VAE encoder is not scale-equivariant. Building on this analysis we introduce MACRO, Multi-plane Attention for Closeup Render Optimization, a training-free method for high-quality close-up novel view synthesis from 3DGS. MACRO resolves the scale gap by leveraging the scene's known 3D structure: it decomposes the close-up into depth planes, crops and resizes references in image space to match the scale of each plane before encoding, and applies a depth-aware attention mask so each token attends only to scale-matched references. The method requires no architectural changes or additional training. We further contribute two new close-up novel view synthesis benchmarks, the first standardized evaluation protocol for this setting, and demonstrate state-of-the-art results on both, outperforming existing 3DGS and diffusion-based methods on both reconstruction and perceptual metrics. Project page: https://nitzanhod.github.io/MACRO</p></details> | Project page: https://nitzanhod.github.io/MACRO |
| **[Stage-wise Lineage-aware Dropout for Sparse-View 3D Gaussian Splatting](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.17752v1)** | 2026-11-13 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>Sparse-view 3D Gaussian Splatting (3DGS) is prone to overfitting because only a limited number of images are available to constrain scene geometry. The founder Gaussians refer to all Gaussian primitives generated during initialization. Descendants within the same family often jointly contribute to the refinement of adjacent local regions. However, dropping individual Gaussians can be less effective due to the compensation effect from neighboring Gaussians for the dropped ones. To address this issue, we propose Stage-wise Lineage-aware Dropout(SLD), which applies lineage-family dropout during the structural regularization stage and switches to individual dropout during the later refinement stage. We further propose Lineage-Balanced Densification(LBD), which operates during densification by constructing an expanded candidate pool and reallocating the densification budget according to the mean gradient scores and existing descendant sizes of different families. Experiments on the LLFF, MipNeRF360, and Blender datasets show that the proposed framework achieves competitive performance in sparse-view reconstruction</p></details> | 11 pages, 9 figures, 7 tables, including supplementary material |
| **[DPGS-SLAM: Diffusion-Prior and Provenance-Aware Monocular Gaussian Splatting SLAM for Dynamic Visual Sensing](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.12038v2)** | 2026-11-12 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>This paper presents DPGS-SLAM (Diffusion-Prior and Provenance-Aware Monocular Gaussian Splatting SLAM) for dense mapping in environments containing dynamic objects. When previously occluded backgrounds become visible, eligible generated primitives are removed, and observation-supported primitives are independently initialized from real RGB observations and predicted depth. Experimental results demonstrated competitive camera tracking, with average ATE RMSE values of 1.2 cm on TUM RGB-D and 2.7 cm on BONN RGB-D datasets. On StaDy4D, DPGS-SLAM improved average PSNR by 4.60 dB over WildGS-SLAM using only monocular RGB input.</p></details> |  |
| **[GC-MoE on SM120: Overlapping Expert-Parallel Token Dispatch with Warp-Level FP8xFP4 Block-Scaled MMA on Consumer Blackwell](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.16219v1)** | 2026-11-12 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>Expert-parallel Mixture-of-Experts decoding is latency bound: each layer serializes a token exchange, a grouped GEMM over routed experts, and a combine, while only a few tokens per rank are in flight. SM120 exposes block-scaled tensor cores only through warp-level mma.sync.aligned.kind::mxf8f6f4: no tensor memory, no two-CTA MMA, no cluster multicast. We rebuild the kernel around a swap-AB warp-level block-scaled MMA with FP4 weights in the register A operand and FP8 activations in B, plus SM120-specific mechanisms: scale-byte selection through MMA immediates, a compact-weights path, an adaptive token-tile geometry, an expert-granular streaming dispatch with a batched bulk-pull engine and a 64-bit deduplicating pull cache, full-width BF16 returns drained by resident relay copiers over a position-major arena, and shared experts folded into the compute role. On 8xRTX PRO 5000 over PCIe the operator is 1.522x and 1.387x faster than the FlashInfer CuTe DSL W4A8 MegaMoE and the Cake MegaMoE across 18 decode points (18/18 wins each), and 1.388x faster than Cake with shared experts fused; all 94 numerical cases and 752 per-rank checks pass, at cross-operator cosine similarity of at least 0.9999997.</p></details> | 24 pages, 10 tables, 6 figures |

| **[Multi-Band Galaxy Novel-View Synthesis with Radiative Gaussian Fields](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.24723v1)** | 2026-11-18 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>We propose a novel view synthesis tool for galaxies based on Gaussian Splatting (GS). Unlike RGB data, where GS has achieved success, galaxy observations consist of multi-band signals from a massive number of bodies, contain complex patterns of cross-band interactions, and capture light propagation in highly heterogeneous media. As a result, the underlying physical process poses great challenges in model representation and optimization, which have not been investigated in existing research. To this end, we propose a physically inspired Gaussian Splatting method, GalaxyGS, which is equipped with physically meaningful components that capture both the energy emission mechanism of different astronomical bodies and the light propagation between them. In the absence of directly comparable baseline methods, we adopt a wide range of possible alternative approaches. Comparisons conclusively demonstrate that GalaxyGS is better in accuracy and physical fidelity across datasets and metrics.</p></details> | 10 pages, 3 figures |
| **[RPC-GS: Gaussian Splatting with native RPC Rendering for Satellite Imagery](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.06690v2)** | 2026-11-18 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>We present RPC-GS, the first Gaussian Splatting framework for satellite imagery that operates natively with Rational Polynomial Camera (RPC) models. The RPC model is the de facto standard for representing the complex imaging geometry of modern pushbroom satellite sensors. To simplify rendering, prior satellite Gaussian Splatting methods replace the RPC model with perspective or affine camera approximations, leading to geometric errors during reconstruction. RPC-GS avoids these approximations by projecting Gaussian means and covariances directly through the RPC model during the splatting process. We embed the RPC model in a chain of carefully selected geo-coordinate transformations representing a mapping from splatting-suitable scene coordinates to image coordinates. To map the Gaussian covariance matrices, we derive a numerically robust Jacobian-based covariance projection for the (partially nonlinear) coordinate transformations. Since RPCs lack an explicit notion of camera depth, we integrate a metric ray-based depth formulation. We benchmark RPC, perspective, and affine camera models in a unified framework, with our native RPC renderer achieving the lowest reconstruction error on leading satellite benchmark datasets, improving mean altitude error over perspective and affine approximations by 34.3% and 13.5% on DFC2019, and by 5.0% and 1.2% on IARPA2016. Our implementation is publicly available at https://github.com/wagnva/RPC-GS.</p></details> | Accepted at NeurIPS 2026 |
| **[DAL: Dynamic Angular Loss for Imbalanced Medical Image Classification](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.24597v1)** | 2026-11-18 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>Class imbalance remains a major obstacle in medical image classification, where rare but clinically important classes are often overshadowed by majority categories. Although current strategies for reducing bias, including angle-based softmax (ABS) losses, offer strong discriminative features, their behavior in settings with a small number of severely imbalanced classes remains insufficiently understood. Previous approaches that estimate the intrinsic parameters of ABS rely on the assumption of many classes, which does not hold in medical settings. We derive data-driven upper and lower bounds for the scale parameter and an upper bound for the angular margin using negative-angle statistics, and use these to drive a batch-adaptive angular margin (BAAM), which we combine with dynamically-weighted cross entropy (DWCE) to define our dynamic angular loss (DAL). Across four highly imbalanced medical image classification benchmarks spanning dermatology, ophthalmology, and gastrointestinal endoscopy, our method consistently achieves superior balanced accuracy and the lowest coefficient of variation of per-class recall. Although this entails a small performance reduction on majority classes, the gains on minority categories are substantial, yielding a reliable and equitable overall classifier. Our source code is available at: https://github.com/msalman95/DAL.</p></details> | Accepted at the British Machine Vision Conference (BMVC) 2026. 18 pages, 5 figures, 4 tables. Code: https://github.com/msalman95/DA |
| **[RAPID: Reconstruction Accelerated by Polarization-Informed Depth for 2D Gaussian Splatting](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.24404v1)** | 2026-11-18 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>Efficient and accurate 3D surface reconstruction from multi-view images remains a fundamental challenge in Gaussian splatting. Many existing methods rely on sparse geometric initialization and iterative densification, resulting in substantial optimization overhead and difficulties in balancing rendering quality and geometric accuracy, particularly in reflective and weakly textured regions. To address these limitations, we propose RAPID, a geometry-first framework that uses multiview polarization cues to recover dense surface priors. We integrate surface normals into depth maps using sparse geometric anchors. These depth maps are then fused across views. Curvature-adaptive sampling and oriented Gaussian initialization then support efficient optimization. Experiments on the PISR dataset demonstrate an approximately \(5\times\) speedup over PhyGaP, which is a state-of-the-art Gaussian-based reconstruction method. RAPID reduces the average optimization time from approximately 21 minutes (PhyGaP) to 4 minutes while maintaining competitive novel-view synthesis quality and mesh reconstruction accuracy. These results demonstrate the effectiveness of RAPID in accelerating Gaussian optimization from polarimetric multi-view inputs.</p></details> | 10 pages, 5 figures, 5tables |
| **[Spectral World Renderer: Unified 3D Hyperspectral Unmixing](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.19520v2)** | 2026-11-18 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>Hyperspectral images (HSI) preserve material evidence lost in RGB, yet 3D material identification from multi-view HSI faces two obstacles. Additionally, appearance guidance prevents spectrally similar materials from merging when intensity absorbs their brightness differences. Moreover, SWR requires no material-label supervision during training. To address the data gap, we further construct the SWR Benchmark (SWR-Bench): a coding-agent pipeline generates eight indoor and outdoor worlds from measured reflectance, aligning HSI with both annotations. SWR achieves the highest mIoU in all eight worlds and the highest mean PSNR on each real dataset. | **[NIRJALA: Draining the Sea out of Gaussian Splatting with Three-Path Optics and Reversible Physics](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.23450v1)** | 2026-11-17 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>Water does not just tint an underwater photograph; it rewrites it. Each pixel mixes attenuated scene radiance, backscattered light, and scene light scattered forward on its way to the camera, and many combinations of scene and water explain the same pixels. PRATIBIMBA, a reversible physics anchor, ties a flexible neural water model to a ten-parameter physical model in both directions, so scene content cannot hide inside the water. SAMARASA collects the physics-informed objectives, including a range-binned colour prior. To score water removal against ground truth, we built SAJALA ("with water"), a 30-scene suite path-traced in Blender Cycles with water-free images, depth, and normals under controlled water colour and turbidity. On SAJALA, NIRJALA raises PSNR from 24.34 to 24.76 dB and lowers depth AbsRel from 0.109 to 0.091 against the strongest baseline; on real SeaThru-NeRF scenes it lowers LPIPS from 0.194 to 0.175 while rendering at 44 FPS. To address this gap, we propose AgriSplat, which relaxes visibility filtering during initialization and reallocates model capacity through contribution-based pruning and budgeted reinvestment in sub-threshold candidates ranked by existing gradient statistics. After reallocation, asymmetric directional supervision penalizes insufficient contrast along reference image-gradient directions. Relative to Octree-GS, AgriSplat improves mean crop-region gradient-energy ratio from 0.506 to 0.847 and edge F-score from 0.712 to 0.797 across seven scenarios, while also improving whole-image PSNR, SSIM, and LPIPS. These contributions provide a dataset, benchmark, and method for systematic crop reconstruction under field conditions.</p></details> | Keywords: Agricultural robotics, Multimodal dataset, Longitudinal crop monitoring, Illumination variability, 3D Gaussian Splatting, Novel view synthesis, 3D Reconstruction |
| **[SPAR: Placement-aware Self-Supervised Representation Learning for Distributed Sensing](https://arxiv.org/abs/2505.16936v5)** | 2026-11-13 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>To support distributed sensing applications that require understanding of entities in the environment and their locations, this paper develops a self-supervised approach for placement-aware representation learning in spatially-distributed multi-sensor systems. The goal is to produce a latent representation of the physical environment that decouples upstream sensor placement from downstream analytics (in the sense that the pretrained encoder is reused without retraining when sensor placement changes), while supporting spatial reasoning tasks, such as joint target localization and classification, that are sensitive to sensor placement. A challenge lies in the multiplicity of sensing vantage points that collectively offer information on the shared physical environment. The dependence of measurements on sensor locations implies that the latent representation must jointly encode both the observed signals and observer vantage points, doing so in a way that supports the downstream tasks. Moreover, since the exact deployment-time sensor placement is generally not known at training time, the representation learning framework must be pre-trained to produce such a representation without (training-time) knowledge of deployment-time placement. This challenge distinguishes our goal from approaches, such as NeRF and Gaussian Splatting, that are trained to represent each particular environment given a specific placement of observation points in that environment. In contrast, our representation learning solution, called SPAR, pretrains an encoder independently of future placement by utilizing a novel training objective that emphasizes the duality between signals and vantage points...</p></details> |  |
| **[MacroGS: Macro-Pixel Shared Gaussian Primitives for Arbitrary-Scale Light Field Super-Resolutio](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.17957v1)** | 2026-11-13 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>Arbitrary-scale light field spatial super-resolution reconstructs high-resolution views at any requested scale from low-resolution multi-view observations. Gaussian primitives enable continuous reconstruction, but generating separate primitive sets for each view redundantly encodes shared scene structure across views and causes the primitive count grow linearly with the number of views. To avoid such redundancy, we propose MacroGS, a feed-forward decoder that jointly predicts a single shared Gaussian set per macro pixel rather than generating separate sets for each view. Disparity-dependent replacement and angle-conditioned appearance allow each set to be reused across all output views, keeping the predicted primitive count independent of the number of views at each requested scale. The decoder iteratively predicts per-primitive disparities, retrieves the corresponding multi-view features, and updates the primitive states. Gaussian splatting and residual refinement then reconstruct the output light field. Despite being learned without geometric supervision, the predicted disparities strongly correlate with underlying scene geometry. Experiments across five benchmarks demonstrate that MacroGS integrates effectively with diverse light field encoders, improving reconstruction quality at fixed scales while achieving leading performance across most evaluated arbitrary-scale settings. The decoder also achieves speedups of approximately $2$-$10\times$ over state-of-the-art implicit light field decoders and $10$-$18\times$ over the view-wise Gaussian baseline.</p></details> | 11 pages, 5 figures |
| **[Stage-wise Lineage-aware Dropout for Sparse-View 3D Gaussian Splatting](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.17752v1)** | 2026-11-13 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>Sparse-view 3D Gaussian Splatting (3DGS) is prone to overfitting because only a limited number of images are available to constrain scene geometry. Dropout is one regularization strategy for alleviating this problem. We introduce the concept of a lineage family to describe a set of Gaussian primitives derived from the same founder through cloning or splitting operations. The founder Gaussians refer to all Gaussian primitives generated during initialization. Descendants within the same family often jointly contribute to the refinement of adjacent local regions. However, dropping individual Gaussians can be less effective due to the compensation effect from neighboring Gaussians for the dropped ones. To address this issue, we propose Stage-wise Lineage-aware Dropout(SLD), which applies lineage-family dropout during the structural regularization stage and switches to individual dropout during the later refinement stage. We further propose Lineage-Balanced Densification(LBD), which operates during densification by constructing an expanded candidate pool and reallocating the densification budget according to the mean gradient scores and existing descendant sizes of different families. Experiments on the LLFF, MipNeRF360, and Blender datasets show that the proposed framework achieves competitive performance in sparse-view reconstruction</p></details> | 11 pages, 9 figures, 7 tables, including supplementary material |
| **[MedGS: Gaussian Splatting for Multi-Modal 3D Medical Imaging](https://arxiv.org/abs/2509.16806v5)** | 2026-11-12 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>Multi-modal three-dimensional (3D) medical imaging data, derived from ultrasound, magnetic resonance imaging (MRI), and computed tomography (CT), provide a widely adopted approach for non-invasive anatomical visualization. However, accurate modeling depends on surface reconstruction and frame-to-frame interpolation, where traditional methods often struggle with image noise and incomplete information between sparse frames. To address these challenges, we present \our{}, a novel framework based on Gaussian Splatting (GS) designed for high-fidelity 3D anatomical reconstruction. Uniquely, \our{} employs a multi-task architecture that simultaneously performs frame interpolation and segmentation using a unified geometric representation. By coupling these tasks, the model leverages dense signals from image synthesis to regularize the geometry, enabling high-quality surface extraction even from a limited number of input frames. Specifically, medical data are modeled as Folded-Gaussians with dual color attributes, supported by an In-Between Frame Regularization (IBFR) mechanism. Experimental results demonstrate that \our{} achieves higher metric scores than implicit neural representations and improves interpolation quality. Repository: https://github.com/gmum/MedGS</p></details> |  |

## Gaussian rasterization
| **Title** | **Date** | **Abstract** | **Comment** |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| **[DAL: Dynamic Angular Loss for Imbalanced Medical Image Classification](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.24597v1)** | 2026-11-18 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>Class imbalance remains a major obstacle in medical image classification, where rare but clinically important classes are often overshadowed by majority categories. Although current strategies for reducing bias, including angle-based softmax (ABS) losses, offer strong discriminative features, their behavior in settings with a small number of severely imbalanced classes remains insufficiently understood. Previous approaches that estimate the intrinsic parameters of ABS rely on the assumption of many classes, which does not hold in medical settings. We derive data-driven upper and lower bounds for the scale parameter and an upper bound for the angular margin using negative-angle statistics, and use these to drive a batch-adaptive angular margin (BAAM), which we combine with dynamically-weighted cross entropy (DWCE) to define our dynamic angular loss (DAL). Across four highly imbalanced medical image classification benchmarks spanning dermatology, ophthalmology, and gastrointestinal endoscopy, our method consistently achieves superior balanced accuracy and the lowest coefficient of variation of per-class recall. Although this entails a small performance reduction on majority classes, the gains on minority categories are substantial, yielding a reliable and equitable overall classifier. Our source code is available at: https://github.com/msalman95/DAL.</p></details> | Accepted at the British Machine Vision Conference (BMVC) 2026. 18 pages, 5 figures, 4 tables. Code: https://github.com/msalman95/DA |
| **[TRUCO: A Scalable Lock-Free Algorithm for Parallel Contour Extraction](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.37297v3)** | 2026-11-15 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>Extracting binary contours and their containment hierarchy is a fundamental step in many computer vision pipelines. While the standard Suzuki--Abe algorithm is inherently sequential, existing parallel tracers neither preserve its exact contours nor compute their hierarchy. This paper presents TRUCO (Threaded Raster Unrestricted Contour Ownership), a lock-free parallel algorithm for multi-core CPUs that produces the exact output of Suzuki--Abe. TRUCO relies on two core ideas. First, the image is partitioned into horizontal stripes, and a geometric ownership rule allows each thread to trace complete contours independently across stripe boundaries, avoiding both locks and post-processing merges. Second, contour tracing is decoupled from hierarchy reconstruction, which is performed in a subsequent parallel pass and can be skipped entirely when only the contours are needed. We formally prove that TRUCO is deterministic, data-race-free, and identical in output to Suzuki--Abe regardless of thread count. Across diverse high-resolution datasets-from sparse segmentation masks to dense real-world photographs-TRUCO achieves speedups of 4.7x to 17.4x over the fastest sequential baseline with hierarchy computation, and 2.3x to 17.8x without it.</p></details> |  |
| **[GC-MoE on SM120: Overlapping Expert-Parallel Token Dispatch with Warp-Level FP8xFP4 Block-Scaled MMA on Consumer Blackwell](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.16219v1)** | 2026-11-12 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>Expert-parallel Mixture-of-Experts decoding is latency bound: each layer serializes a token exchange, a grouped GEMM over routed experts, and a combine, while only a few tokens per rank are in flight. Prior GC-MoE fused these phases into one persistent, Green-Context-partitioned CUDA Graph on Hopper (SM90) and datacenter Blackwell (SM100). This paper rebuilds the operator for SM120 (compute capability 12.0, e.g. RTX PRO 5000) over PCIe. SM120 exposes block-scaled tensor cores only through warp-level mma.sync.aligned.kind::mxf8f6f4: no tensor memory, no two-CTA MMA, no cluster multicast. We rebuild the kernel around a swap-AB warp-level block-scaled MMA with FP4 weights in the register A operand and FP8 activations in B, plus SM120-specific mechanisms: scale-byte selection through MMA immediates, a compact-weights path, an adaptive token-tile geometry, an expert-granular streaming dispatch with a batched bulk-pull engine and a 64-bit deduplicating pull cache, full-width BF16 returns drained by resident relay copiers over a position-major arena, and shared experts folded into the compute role. On 8xRTX PRO 5000 over PCIe the operator is 1.522x and 1.387x faster than the FlashInfer CuTe DSL W4A8 MegaMoE and the Cake MegaMoE across 18 decode points (18/18 wins each), and 1.388x faster than Cake with shared experts fused; all 94 numerical cases and 752 per-rank checks pass, at cross-operator cosine similarity of at least 0.9999997.</p></details> | 24 pages, 10 tables, 6 figures |
| **[LILA: Data-Free Structured Pruning of LLMs via Latent Spectral Geometry](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.11163v2)** | 2026-11-11 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>Structured pruning of large language models (LLMs) has universally required calibration data, gradient computation, or large auxiliary policy networks at pruning time. LILA (\emph{Latent-Informed Layer Analysis}) demonstrates that this dependency is avoidable: each FFN weight matrix is factorized with Non-negative Matrix Factorization (NMF) to obtain a parts-based spectral representation, and neuron importance is scored via the Kolmogorov--Smirnov (KS) distance between singular value distributions of the full and neuron-ablated NMF matrix, a closed-form criterion requiring no calibration corpus, forward pass, or gradient computation. Two data-free matrix variants (Abs, Split) generate pruning masks from weight spectral geometry alone; a calibration-guided variant (Act) is provided as an internal ablation. Evaluation spans 7 model families (up to 70B). Without fine-tuning, data-free LILA surpasses PruneNet (45M-parameter RL policy) by up to 2.12~pp in zero-shot accuracy on LLaMA-2-7B and outperforms WikiText-2-calibrated SliceGPT by up to 6.1~pp across all sparsity levels, confirming data-free performance is not an artifact of omitting activation statistics. After one LoRA RFT epoch (matched WikiText-2 protocol), LILA Act surpasses SliceGPT+RFT by 6.39~pp on average using 98.4\% fewer calibration samples (16 vs.\ 1,024) across LLaMA-2-7B and Phi-2. NTK analysis confirms a $17{\times}$ reduction in functional distortion versus random pruning, providing theoretical grounding for the spectral criterion.</p></details> | 31 Pages |
| **[A Multi-Parent Edge Assembly Crossover Driven Hybrid Genetic Algorithm for the Hamiltonian \textit{p}-Median Problem](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.15219v1)** | 2026-11-11 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>The Hamiltonian \textit{p}-median problem seeks to partition the vertices of a graph into exactly $p$ disjoint cycles while minimizing their total cost. This paper presents a hybrid genetic algorithm incorporating a multi-parent edge assembly crossover operator. The crossover combines a base solution with multiple donor solutions in a single recombination operation, thereby expanding the space of available edge exchanges. It uses virtual copies of the base solution to establish edge balance, together with an auxiliary directed graph and a budgeted search to construct valid AB-cycles. Extensive experiments on 215 benchmark instances across four sets show that the proposed algorithm improves the best-known solution values for 34 instances and matches those for another 168. Additional experiments indicate that a moderate number of parents provides beneficial diversity, whereas excessive parent counts, particularly when $p$ is large, lead to excessive population dispersion and hinder convergence.</p></details> |  |
| **[An exact compress-with-another threshold in a binary automaton family](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.07044v2)** | 2026-11-10 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>For a state $q$ in a synchronizing automaton, let $μ(q)$ be the minimum length of a word merging $q$ with another initial state. Szykuła asked whether $μ(q)=O(n)$ for strongly connected synchronizing automata and described a binary family attributed to Dżyga, with $n=3p$ states and a conjectured threshold $4n/3$. We specify the boundary transitions omitted from the published piecewise definition and prove that the resulting automata $\mathcal A_p$ satisfy $μ(q_0)=4p=4n/3$ for all $p\geq3$. A shortest word merging $q_0$ with another state is $(ba)^p(ab)^p$. We also construct a reset word of length $3p^2+4p-1$. Consequently, any uniform linear upper bound for $μ(q)$ on binary strongly connected synchronizing automata with corank-one letters has leading constant at least $4/3$.</p></details> | 9 pages, 1 figure. Revised proofs and exposition, corrected boundary transitions, updated references, and added a coauthor |
| **[Ground-Wing: A Parametric 3D Wing-in-Ground-Effect Dataset for Benchmarking Neural Surrogate Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.04571v2)** | 2026-11-09 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>This paper presents a CFD dataset of a highly parametrized 3D wing operating in ground effect, designed for benchmarking neural surrogate models for aerodynamic prediction, using a wing shape and flow conditions typically encountered in motorsport applications. The dataset comprises 770 RANS simulations of 194 unique wing configurations, sampled across 27 airfoil profile variants and 4 distances from the ground, spanning variations in wing sweep, chord, taper, angle of attack, and tip twist. Six neural network architectures representing distinct paradigms --- convolutional (DoMINO), graph-based (X-MeshGraphNet), transformer-based (AB-UPT, GeoTransolver, GAOT), and JEPA-based (AeroJEPA) --- are evaluated on their ability to predict surface pressure coefficient and wall shear stress distributions, from which integral drag and lift coefficients are recovered. The dataset is intended to be used as a challenging benchmark for surrogate CFD models, due to the uniqueness of the ground effect being absent from other wing and airfoil datasets. The dataset is available for download at https://huggingface.co/datasets/andreacenzato/Ground-Wing</p></details> |  |

Code: https://github.com/msalman95/DA |
| **[QuantileGS: Quantile-Based Conformal Bounds on RGB Rendering Errors for Novel View Synthesis with 3D Gaussian Splatting](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.21253v1)** | 2026-11-16 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>Uncertainty quantification for novel view synthesis with 3D Gaussian Splatting (3DGS) assesses rendering reliability at unseen viewpoints. This reliability depends on the discrepancy between rendered and true colors, which cannot be measured without ground-truth images. We therefore quantify uncertainty in this unknown discrepancy through upper bounds on per-pixel RGB mean absolute error. However, learned bounds may underestimate these errors and overstate rendering reliability, motivating calibration to a prescribed coverage level. In this paper, we propose QuantileGS, a post-hoc framework that combines learned rendering-error quantiles with scale-normalized conformal calibration to produce spatially adaptive error bounds. The framework comprises three phases. Phase 1, Rendering-Error Computation, measures RGB mean absolute error between observed images and predictions from a frozen 3DGS model. Phase 2, Ordered Gaussian Quantile Fields, learns two scalar attributes per Gaussian to predict lower and upper rendering-error quantiles. Phase 3, Scale-Normalized Conformal Calibration, calibrates the upper prediction on held-out images, using the quantile gap to scale the correction. Under the stated assumptions, the resulting bounds provide finite-sample marginal coverage. At a nominal coverage level of 90%, calibration raises mean empirical test coverage from 69.6% to 89.6% on LLFF and from 80.1% to 92.1% on Mip-NeRF 360. On LF, coverage remains conservative at 96.3% before and 96.4% after calibration. Applications to active view selection, Gaussian pruning, and RGB-D simultaneous localization and mapping illustrate how these bounds inform downstream decisions.</p></details> |  |
